> Historical mechanism Source: this document remains controlling for Long-Task non-UI semantic Fact/Obligation closure. [`workflow-assurance-boundary.md`](workflow-assurance-boundary.md) supersedes only the clauses that previously required the default Workflow Contract to reproduce that exact production-conformance accounting ephemerally; it does not weaken or revise any Long-Task Source, Contract, Authority, evidence or Final-Gate rule below.

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=semantic-fact-completeness-result kind=outcome_result -->
Formal Long-Task completes non-UI meaning at the finest independently decidable semantic Fact and applicable-condition granularity: all material Source meaning is inventoried, missing implementation decisions are completed within authority or blocked, and exact Facts and proof obligations are conserved through Contract and one current Final Gate. The default Workflow preserves the same Source authority but uses model-led, risk-proportional requirement understanding and evidence-bounded reporting rather than that exact carrier; neither route may silently invent or omit an important in-scope product, business, data, state, interface, quality, security, privacy or operational decision.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=default-fine-grained-non-ui-authoring kind=requirement -->
Long-Task Source authoring must default to completing non-UI requirements at the finest independently decidable semantic-fact granularity before implementation, rather than treating a paragraph, Requirement, Product Control, acceptance scenario, broad status catalogue, or current code shape as the granularity ceiling.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=authority-provenance-and-decision-boundary kind=requirement -->
Every added semantic fact must be classified as direct, logically derived, explicitly delegated, evidence-backed preservation, or decision-required; user and project authority remain controlling, implementation code cannot silently create intent, and a real product, legal, security, commercial, safety, or externally owned choice remains blocking until its authority is supplied.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=material-input-and-basis-closure kind=requirement -->
Authoring must inventory every material request fragment, attachment, Context unit, canonical schema or specification, selected external constraint, repository preservation source, and delegated instruction with immutable identity and disposition, then bind every generated fact, exclusion, equivalence, assumption, and conflict to exact basis references so an unread or unclassified input cannot disappear.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=standard-and-custom-domain-family-closure kind=requirement -->
The Expected Semantic Fact Universe must explicitly disposition every standard semantic family and every scoped custom family as applicable, not-applicable with basis, decision-required, or unavailable; a fixed catalogue is a completeness floor rather than a ceiling, and domain-specific obligations discovered from Source or repository structures must extend it.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=subject-census-and-stable-identity kind=requirement -->
The semantic census must give stable independently queryable identities to every applicable outcome, actor, role, tenant or organization, entitlement, domain entity, field, relation, command, query, workflow, state machine, state, transition, policy, interface operation, message, event, job, integration, store, index, transaction, cache, configuration, flag, migration, deployment, dependency, security or privacy boundary, SLO, telemetry signal, alert, runbook, AI component, device component, and custom semantic unit.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=relation-hierarchy-and-population-closure kind=requirement -->
Parent-child, instance-of, ownership, cardinality, dependency, ordering, causality, synchronization, derivation, source-of-truth, producer-consumer, authorization, containment, cross-surface, and other multi-subject relations must be first-class facts with typed endpoint roles; static and dynamic populations require exact universe identity, enumeration or partition rules, exclusions, and set-equal observed coverage rather than one representative item.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=goal-scope-glossary-assumption-and-decision-closure kind=requirement -->
Authoring must atomically preserve goals, user or system outcomes, scope, non-goals, glossary terms, units, invariants, assumptions, delegated choices, unresolved decisions, conflicts, priorities, and acceptance meaning, including which statements are normative, informative, current-state evidence, or intentionally outside this delivery.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=actor-role-tenant-entitlement-and-responsibility kind=requirement -->
Every applicable actor, role, tenant, organization, plan, entitlement, ownership and responsibility fact must state identity, eligibility, visibility, allowed and forbidden operations, delegation or impersonation rules, isolation boundary, approval owner, escalation path, and lifecycle under each applicable condition.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=business-rule-decision-table-and-calculation kind=requirement -->
Business rules must be decomposed into atomic predicates, inputs, precedence, complete decision-table branches, overlap and gap handling, outputs, calculations, formula versions, units, precision, rounding, thresholds, inclusivity, caps, floors, exceptions, effective dates, and audit rationale so no branch or arithmetic choice is left to implementation inference.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=data-model-field-and-value-semantics kind=requirement -->
Every applicable entity, identifier, field and derived value must specify type, schema, nullability, requiredness, default versus absence, enum domain, uniqueness, cardinality, ownership, provenance, lineage, mutability, lifecycle, unit, precision, scale, rounding, format, encoding, normalization, ordering, comparison, invariant, validation boundary, sensitivity, retention and serialization semantics.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=operation-workflow-and-side-effect-semantics kind=requirement -->
Every command, query and workflow must atomically state trigger, initiator, target, preconditions, inputs, guards, ordered steps, state transitions, synchronous and asynchronous side effects, outputs, postconditions, partial success, cancellation, replacement, compensation, feedback, audit events, and forbidden effects for success, degradation and recovery paths.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=state-machine-and-lifecycle-semantics kind=requirement -->
Every lifecycle must enumerate states, initial and terminal states, transitions, triggers, guards, actions, emitted effects, illegal transitions, compound and concurrent states, precedence, re-entry, interruption, cancellation, timeout, stale and conflict handling, recovery, restoration and archival or deletion behavior; one all-states label cannot substitute for atomic rows.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=temporal-scheduling-and-clock-semantics kind=requirement -->
Time-dependent facts must state clock source and monotonicity, timezone, locale calendar, DST gaps and folds, clock skew, timestamps, effective and expiry boundaries, inclusive or exclusive intervals, schedules, recurrence, grace periods, debounce and throttle windows, ordering, lateness, timeout, lease and retry timing, and deterministic test-clock behavior.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=input-validation-normalization-and-boundary kind=requirement -->
Each input channel and field must declare source, type, requiredness, default and absence, accepted range or grammar, units, encoding, normalization, canonicalization, cross-field rules, validation order and location, duplicate and replay behavior, size and rate limits, malformed and adversarial cases, error identity, user or caller feedback, and recovery without data loss.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=output-error-feedback-and-partial-success kind=requirement -->
Each output, response, result, notification and externally visible effect must declare schema, ordering, formatting, provenance, freshness, pagination or streaming semantics, success, empty, partial, stale, degraded and error variants, stable error taxonomy and codes, retryability, caller-visible feedback, correlation identity, redaction, and reconciliation or recovery behavior.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=api-protocol-and-versioning kind=requirement -->
Every API or protocol operation must specify endpoint or method identity, transport, authentication, authorization, request parameters, headers and body, serialization and content negotiation, response and error variants, status codes, pagination, filtering, sorting, idempotency, rate and quota semantics, timeout, retry, callbacks or webhooks, version negotiation, deprecation and compatibility rules.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=event-message-stream-and-job kind=requirement -->
Every event, message, stream and background job must specify producer, consumer, schema and version, partition or routing key, correlation and causation, ordering, delivery guarantee, acknowledgement, deduplication, idempotency, retry and backoff, timeout, dead-letter handling, replay, retention, backpressure, poison messages, cancellation, scheduling and observable completion.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=persistence-cache-search-and-data-lifecycle kind=requirement -->
Storage, cache, search and derived-index facts must state canonical owner, schema, keys and indexes, query and ordering semantics, transaction boundary, persistence and eviction, freshness and invalidation, replication, archival, retention, deletion and tombstones, import and export, backup participation, search indexing, tokenization, ranking, filters, pagination and rebuild or reconciliation behavior.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=transaction-consistency-concurrency-idempotency kind=requirement -->
Transactional and distributed behavior must specify atomicity, isolation, consistency model, durability, read and write visibility, locking or lease ownership, optimistic and pessimistic conflicts, race and interleaving cases, ordering and causality, duplicate and replay handling, idempotency scope and key lifetime, retry safety, lost update prevention, partial commit, compensation and convergence.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=fault-timeout-retry-degradation-recovery kind=requirement -->
Every independently failing boundary must enumerate fault classes, detection, timeout budget, retry eligibility and schedule, jitter, retry exhaustion, circuit breaker or bulkhead behavior, fallback and degraded result, cancellation, cleanup, compensation, user or caller feedback, recovery trigger, restoration point, reconciliation and proof that recovery does not conceal data loss or duplicate effects.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=backup-restore-disaster-recovery kind=requirement -->
Persistent and critical state must explicitly disposition backup scope, frequency, consistency point, encryption and retention, restore procedure, verification, RPO, RTO, regional or dependency disaster cases, failover and failback, replay and reconciliation, ownership and drill evidence; a backup-created signal cannot substitute for a successful restore observation.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=configuration-feature-flag-secret-and-precedence kind=requirement -->
Configuration, feature flags and secret references must declare schema, type, requiredness, safe default, scope, environment and tenant precedence, override and conflict rules, validation, reload or restart behavior, rollout and cohort assignment, kill switch, expiry and cleanup, audit, protected representation, rotation and failure behavior without embedding secret values in Source, logs or evidence.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=compatibility-migration-rollout-and-rollback kind=requirement -->
Compatibility and evolution facts must cover supported platform, runtime, client, API, schema, storage and protocol versions; backward, forward and mixed-version behavior; migration, backfill, validation, dual-read or dual-write, cutover, canary or phased rollout, downgrade, rollback, irreversible boundary, cleanup, deprecation, data reconciliation and failure recovery.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=performance-capacity-resource-and-cost kind=requirement -->
Performance and capacity requirements must name the user or system journey, workload model, dataset and cardinality, payload, concurrency, warm or cold state, latency percentile or deadline, throughput, queue and backpressure limits, resource budgets, saturation and scaling behavior, cost or quota boundary, benchmark environment, measurement window, statistical method and tolerated variance.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=reliability-availability-freshness-and-slo kind=requirement -->
Reliability facts must define the user-valued service indicator, measurement point, valid population and exclusions, target and window, availability, correctness, durability, freshness or continuity objective, error budget or failure allowance, dependency assumptions, degraded-mode expectation, alerting relationship and recovery response instead of a context-free uptime percentage.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=security-trust-authn-authz-session-and-abuse kind=requirement -->
Security facts must cover assets and threats, trust boundaries, authentication, identity proofing, authorization and least privilege, tenant isolation, sessions and token lifecycle, MFA and recovery, secrets and key rotation, encryption and integrity in transit and at rest, input and output defenses, audit, rate limiting and abuse cases, dependency and supply-chain integrity, vulnerability handling and incident response.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=privacy-data-governance-and-rights kind=requirement -->
Privacy facts must cover data classification, purpose and lawful or project authority, consent and preference, minimization, collection and sharing, lineage and processors, residency, retention, access, correction, portability, deletion and derived-data effects, redaction and pseudonymization, logging restrictions, breach or incident handling, user rights, policy version and evidence without exposing protected values.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=safety-compliance-human-approval-and-irreversibility kind=requirement -->
Safety, legal and compliance obligations must identify hazards or prohibited outcomes, jurisdiction and policy version, risk controls, safe state, human approval or four-eyes boundary, reversible versus irreversible actions, confirmation and cancellation, emergency stop, audit and accountability, external expert authority, incident escalation and residual risk that cannot be machine-accepted.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=observability-health-alert-and-runbook kind=requirement -->
Operational observability must specify logs, metrics, traces and audit events with stable names, dimensions, units, correlation and causation, sampling and aggregation, redaction, retention and ownership; health, readiness and liveness semantics; alert condition, threshold, window, deduplication, routing and escalation; dashboards and runbook diagnosis, mitigation and recovery steps.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=deployment-topology-startup-shutdown-and-maintenance kind=requirement -->
Deployment and runtime facts must declare environments, regions, zones, topology, replicas and dependency direction, configuration and secret injection, artifact identity and provenance, startup, readiness, liveness and shutdown, data migration order, rollout and rollback, drain and maintenance behavior, autoscaling, health probes, operational ownership and post-deploy verification.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=external-integration-capability-quota-and-reconciliation kind=requirement -->
Each external integration must freeze provider or protocol identity, capability and version, authentication, region and data boundary, quotas and rate limits, licensing or commercial constraint, request and callback contracts, timeout and retry, idempotency, outage and degraded behavior, external side-effect observation, reconciliation, sandbox versus production differences and externally owned confirmations.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=notification-file-media-and-delivery kind=requirement -->
Notification, file and media requirements must specify audience, channel, template and localization, trigger, priority, quiet hours and cadence, deduplication, delivery receipt, retry and unsubscribe; and where applicable file type, MIME, size, dimensions, duration, codec, metadata, scan and quarantine, upload resume, transformation, storage, signed access, fallback, deletion and accessibility alternatives.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=localization-encoding-unit-and-formatting kind=requirement -->
Non-visual localization facts must specify locale and fallback, language and script, Unicode normalization, encoding, collation, case and accent sensitivity, segmentation, plural and grammar rules, timezone and calendar, date and number formatting, currency and exchange-rate source, measurement units and conversion, decimal precision and rounding, identifiers and protocol fields that must remain locale-invariant.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=commercial-billing-metering-and-financial kind=requirement -->
Commercial and financial behavior must specify product or plan identity, entitlement, price and effective version, meter source and aggregation, currency, tax and jurisdiction, precision and rounding, proration, discounts, quota, invoice, payment state, retry, refund and dispute, idempotency, reconciliation, audit and external processor confirmation without assuming unspecified policy.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=hardware-device-sensor-and-physical kind=requirement -->
Hardware, device and physical-world behavior must, when applicable, specify model and firmware, capability discovery, permissions, sensor units, range, resolution, calibration and sampling, orientation and coordinate frame, latency and buffering, connectivity, power, thermal and storage limits, disconnect and degradation, actuator safety, simulation limits and physical-device confirmation.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=ai-ml-model-prompt-evaluation-and-human-oversight kind=requirement -->
AI and ML behavior must, when applicable, specify model and version, prompt and tool contract, input and output schema, context and grounding sources, stochastic controls, confidence or abstention, safety and policy boundaries, bias and quality criteria, explainability, human review and intervention, evaluation population and tolerance, reproducibility, drift monitoring, fallback, privacy, latency, quota and cost.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=architecture-owner-boundary-and-debt kind=requirement -->
Architecture facts must preserve affected owners, unique source of truth and extension points, dependency direction, interfaces, input and output, state, persistence and lifecycle boundaries, failure and recovery, compatibility and migration, selected design and rejected alternatives, future-change challenge, technical-debt disposition, forbidden bypasses and project-owned conformance checks.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=complete-condition-axis-catalog kind=requirement -->
Applicable condition axes must be first-class structured domains, including actor and role, tenant or organization, jurisdiction and residency, environment, region and zone, platform and device, client, API, schema and runtime version, feature and cohort, configuration, entitlement, permission, consent and session, entity and prior state, input class and boundary, data volume, locale and timezone, clock, concurrency and repetition, dependency health, connectivity, failure and retry, consistency and freshness, migration and rollout, topology, threat and operational mode, plus scoped custom axes.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=atomic-applicability-combination-closure kind=requirement -->
Every applicable atomic condition combination must be enumerated or produced by an exact deterministic expansion with set-equal population proof; every excluded combination needs exact predicates, basis and rationale, continuous domains need explicit partitions and boundaries, and invariance or equivalence may share execution only when machine-checkable without losing a distinct fact identity or verdict.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=typed-fact-identity-and-expected-value kind=requirement -->
One semantic Fact must have stable identity across scope, subject or relation, atomic property, applicability cell and expected predicate; it must carry typed value semantics, canonical Source locator and digest, authority mode, basis, sensitivity, required methods and owner, while compound labels and prose collections cannot encode several states, properties or conditions as one row.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=quantifier-range-null-absence-and-equivalence kind=requirement -->
Fact values must make quantifier and domain explicit, including all, any, none, exactly, at-least and at-most semantics; finite set or dynamic population identity; range boundaries and inclusivity; null, missing, empty, unknown and default distinctions; ordering; tolerance; equivalence relation; and exact rules for repeated or derived values.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=fact-proof-obligation-method-catalog kind=requirement -->
Fact identity and proof obligation must remain separate: every Fact expands to all required verification methods, proof surfaces and evidence capabilities such as exact value, schema contract, decision table, invariant, transition or sequence trace, durable roundtrip, boundary or external effect, concurrency schedule, idempotency repetition, fault injection, recovery drill, migration, compatibility, performance, capacity, security, privacy, audit, observability, deployment and custom named TCB methods.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=per-fact-comparator-oracle-environment kind=requirement -->
Every Fact proof obligation must freeze the expected located digest, comparator, exact or tolerance mode, parameters, mask or exclusions, Oracle identity, version and digest or named external TCB, deterministic environment and population definition, observation sensitivity and redaction; implementation-generated expected values, global tolerances and stale environments are forbidden.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=expected-source-contract-runtime-set-equality kind=requirement -->
The formal invariant is exact conservation: Expected Semantic Fact Universe equals canonical Source-indexed Facts equals Contract-indexed Facts, and the expanded Fact-by-required-method obligation set equals Contract proof bindings equals current Final-Gate result rows; missing, extra, duplicate, narrowed, mismatched or orphan identities fail closed.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=per-fact-current-result-and-multiple-observer kind=requirement -->
Current evidence must emit one attributable result for each and only each expected Fact-by-method obligation, with actual observation, actual environment, expected authority, comparison, tolerance or exact mode, Oracle, pass or fail verdict and distinct identity; one aggregate Boolean, report, screenshot, log or test status cannot replace rows, and one Fact may require multiple independent observers.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=furthest-independent-boundary-and-counterfactual kind=requirement -->
Proof must reach the furthest independently failing boundary named by each Fact and use claim-local semantic counterfactuals that change the production carrier while preserving target liveness; parser success, request issuance, self-reported success, artifact existence or monitoring presence cannot prove downstream persistence, delivery, recovery, security or external effect.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=protected-evidence-redaction-and-secrets kind=requirement -->
Protected semantic facts retain canonical ownership and per-Fact lineage while runtime evidence stores only approved digest-only or redacted structured representations with policy identity and raw-persisted false; secret, token, personal, regulated and high-risk values must never be copied into Contract, logs, findings, Context or long-lived artifacts.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=scalable-complete-generation-no-truncation kind=requirement -->
Authoring, validation and evidence may generate, chunk, stream, group storage or reuse exact executable expansion, but expected counts and identity digests must prove complete deterministic reconstruction; token, row, file, artifact, timeout or UI-size limits may not silently truncate, summarize, sample or merge applicable Facts, and exhausted capability becomes unavailable.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=fail-closed-dispositions-conflicts-and-unreadable kind=requirement -->
Every not-applicable, excluded, equivalent, authority-only, external-confirmation, decision-required and unavailable disposition must have exact affected identities, basis, rationale and allowed completion effect; unresolved conflict, ambiguous precedence, unreadable Source, unsupported schema, unowned fact, missing locator, invalid population or acceptance-affecting authority-only claim blocks readiness.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=default-workflow-and-long-task-projection kind=requirement -->
The default Workflow must identify and implement every expressed material requirement at risk-proportional depth, use attributable current-candidate checks, repair failures and report unverified or unresolved scope without stable Fact/Obligation identities or exact set accounting. An explicitly selected Long-Task projects the complete exact closure into its one Source, Contract, Claims, Assertions, typed evidence and Final Gate. These proof carriers are mutually exclusive; the default route creates no result artifact or machine acceptance authority.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=source-authoring-product-and-engineering-guidance kind=requirement -->
Package-managed Product Plan, Development Engineer, Long-Task, retired Source pointer, default Workflow and public guidance must teach the same Source-authority and no-silent-omission boundary while distinguishing proof levels: only Long-Task teaches the complete domain-family/condition closure, Fact/proof equations and exact no-aggregate carrier; default guidance teaches risk-proportional material understanding, current-candidate checks, repair and evidence-bounded handoff.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=canonical-owner-context-projection kind=requirement -->
Durable product, business, API, schema, data, state, security, privacy, architecture, verification and deployment semantics must be projected to their existing owning Context before implementation when required; task-specific exact facts and provenance remain Source, code remains current implementation truth, and Contract stores only bindings rather than becoming a second durable fact source.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=legacy-migration-and-schema-parser-parity kind=requirement -->
The public Long-Task schema, strict parser, compiler, authority materials, revision diagnostics, runtime evidence codec and documentation must change together; older Contracts that lack mandatory semantic closure receive actionable fail-closed migration diagnostics or an explicitly bounded compatibility route, never inferred facts or silent acceptance.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=anti-degradation-and-implementation-freedom kind=requirement -->
The mechanism must first prove coverage is a superset, false negatives are not increased, Authority and fail-closed behavior are non-bypassable, and the complete current final snapshot is still decisive; it must preserve Goal-owned implementation order, methods, cadence and optional agent allocation without adding a scheduler, per-edit Gate, branch, worktree or delegated-report proof.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=honest-theorem-boundary-and-custom-extension kind=requirement -->
The guarantee cannot prove that a user omitted nothing from their mind, infer an arbitrary unstated preference, or mathematically attest every future domain parser or Oracle; it can force complete treatment of expressed, logically entailed, explicitly delegated and evidence-backed facts under declared catalogues and custom extensions, while unsupported or genuinely undecided affected meaning remains unfinished.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=public-distribution-and-cost-closure kind=requirement -->
Stable semantics, implementation, managed workspace copies, package assets, English and Chinese README surfaces, Source mappings, examples, schema, fixtures, affected and Trust routing, critical sentinels and verification Context must remain aligned; the addition creates no persistent runtime inventory or second Gate, and exact generated rows keep cost proportional to the actual fact and proof universe.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=single-source-semantic-manifest kind=technical_obligation aspect=architecture -->
One Source-embedded machine-readable semantic-fact manifest is the task-local canonical index for exact non-UI facts and their Source locators; it extends the existing Source Draft and compiled Authority materials, while existing Context owners retain durable semantics and the existing Contract and Final Gate remain the only delivery and acceptance authorities.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=machine-enforced-semantic-universe kind=technical_obligation -->
The parser and preflight must validate material-input closure, standard and custom family dispositions, subject and relation census, condition domains and combinations, property obligations, typed values, basis and conflict lineage, dynamic populations, expected collection counts and digests, and exact independently derived Fact universe equality without trusting a self-declared complete flag.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=contract-semantic-fact-binding kind=technical_obligation -->
Contract schema and compiler must generate one Claim per semantic Fact, bind every applicability cell and every required proof surface or method to named Assertions and project the canonical expected authority without copying raw values; Source Facts, generated Claims and Contract bindings must be set-equal.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=typed-semantic-fact-runtime-evidence kind=technical_obligation -->
The V3 structured Check payload, evidence codec, Playwright or structured-runner projection, runtime validator, Progress freshness and Final Gate must require set-equal per-Fact-by-method results with actual observation and environment, frozen expected comparison authority, explicit verdict, protected-value handling and no reused indistinguishable evidence.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=semantic-fact-antidegradation-admission kind=technical_obligation -->
Executable counterexamples must reject omitted families, subjects, relations, conditions, values, methods and current results; aggregate catalog strings, sampled applicability, unsupported N/A, stale Oracle or environment, proxy-boundary proof and implementation self-authority, while all existing Source, Claim, design-fact, Authority, final-snapshot and Implementation Freedom sentinels remain passing.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=semantic-fact-distribution-parity kind=technical_obligation -->
Source and package copies, schema and parser, Skills and references, default managed prompt, Context and PROJECT_SPEC, README surfaces, fixture generators, modularity, affected selection, Trust policy and complete suite must be updated through their existing source mappings and verified byte-for-byte where parity applies.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=no-semantic-fact-shortcuts kind=forbidden_shortcut -->
Do not satisfy this delivery with a longer checklist alone, one broad non-UI Requirement, Product Control fields, current code discovery, an all-states or all-errors string, representative or pairwise samples, default-path tests, ungrounded not-applicable labels, implementation-generated expected values, aggregate pass flags, resource hashes, parser success, proxy evidence, copied precise values in Contract, or any second Source, plan, registry, Authority, Gate, lifecycle state, scheduler or implementation-order mandate.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=semantic-inventory-is-not-completion kind=non_completing -->
A complete Source manifest, successful preflight, equal Fact indexes, compiled Contract, generated schema, focused test pass, Progress record, evidence artifact or prose review alone does not complete implementation; only set-equal passing per-Fact current evidence plus every other declared Check in the existing final current-snapshot closure can authorize completion.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=authoring-universe-mutation-ac kind=acceptance -->
Given mixed product, technical, repository and external inputs, when Source authoring derives the non-UI Expected Semantic Fact Universe, then every material input and standard or custom family has an exact disposition and basis; deleting one input, family, census subject, relation, condition axis, property obligation or custom-domain row fails with its stable identity.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=atomic-axis-and-aggregate-rejection-ac kind=acceptance -->
Given twenty-one states, several roles, versions, environments, failure modes and concurrency cases, when applicability is compiled, then each applicable atomic value and combination has structured identity or exact population expansion; replacing them with a compound label, comma-delimited string, representative row, pairwise sample or unproved invariant fails readiness.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=business-data-state-interface-closure-ac kind=acceptance -->
Given business calculations, typed data, a state machine, an API and asynchronous events, when the manifest is validated, then decision branches, units and rounding, field absence and defaults, every transition and illegal path, request and response or error variants, delivery, ordering, retry and replay facts are independently queryable; removing any one fails closed.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=concurrency-failure-migration-quality-ac kind=acceptance -->
Given concurrent writes, retries, partial failure, migration and explicit latency, capacity and reliability targets, when proof obligations compile, then atomicity, isolation, idempotency, interleavings, timeout and recovery, mixed-version and rollback, workload, percentile, population, measurement and SLO conditions each retain distinct facts and required methods.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=security-privacy-safety-operations-ac kind=acceptance -->
Given authentication and authorization, protected data, an irreversible external action and a deployed service, when closure runs, then trust, tenant, session and key boundaries, purpose, consent, retention and rights, human approval and residual external confirmation, telemetry redaction, health, alert, topology, rollout and recovery facts are all explicit or blocking.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=custom-hardware-ai-domain-extension-ac kind=acceptance -->
Given a delivery involving sensors and an AI model plus one domain-specific rule absent from the standard catalogue, when authoring runs, then calibration, sampling and disconnect behavior, model, prompt, grounding, stochastic tolerance, evaluation and human oversight, and the custom family all extend the universe; catalogue silence cannot mark them complete.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=source-contract-obligation-set-equality-ac kind=acceptance -->
Given a valid Source semantic manifest with Facts requiring multiple methods, when Long-Task compiles, then Expected, Source-indexed and Contract-indexed Fact sets are equal and every Fact-by-method obligation maps to one exact Claim, applicability cell, proof surface and Assertion; missing, extra, duplicate, mismatched or orphan rows block Authority Lock.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=per-fact-current-evidence-ac kind=acceptance -->
Given compiled semantic Fact expectations and current project execution, when structured evidence is decoded, then each obligation has one attributable actual observation and environment, expected locator and digest, comparator and parameters, exact or tolerance mode, Oracle, protection policy and passing verdict; aggregate, reused, stale, failed or fact-free evidence proves nothing.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=furthest-boundary-and-counterfactual-ac kind=acceptance -->
Given facts whose observable outcomes cross persistence, messaging, recovery and external-effect boundaries, when their narrow production carriers are counterfactually changed, then only the designated Fact Assertions fail while target liveness remains, and a nearer proxy or self-reported success cannot satisfy the farther independently failing boundary.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=no-truncation-disposition-bypass-ac kind=acceptance -->
Given a generated universe larger than one chunk with applicable, genuinely non-applicable, decision-required and unavailable cells, when generation and preflight run, then exact counts and identity digests reconstruct all rows, every disposition has exact basis and completion effect, and truncation, summary replacement, sampling, ungrounded N/A or hiding blocked rows fails.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=antidegradation-and-parity-ac kind=acceptance -->
Given the final candidate, when focused manifest, Contract, evidence and guidance tests, the reviewed critical sentinel, affected and Trust routing, complete package suite, package source parity, Context validation, Harness validation and self-hosted Final Gate execute, then every new omission counterexample fails, all prior invariants pass, generated copies align and no second authority or Implementation Freedom restriction exists.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=semantic-public-schema-risk kind=risk_fact fact=public_api_or_schema_change outcome=semantic-fact-completeness -->
The Long-Task V2 public schema, Source formal-block grammar, compiled authority and structured evidence payload gain mandatory semantic-fact closure before release, so strict parser and schema parity, migration diagnostics, examples and package distribution must change together.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=semantic-false-completion-risk kind=risk_fact fact=critical_user_path outcome=semantic-fact-completeness -->
Non-UI semantic closure is a false-completion-critical path because coarse Requirements can omit a business branch, field rule, state transition, interface variant, race, recovery, security, privacy, migration or quality condition while every declared broad assertion still passes.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->

<!-- ty-source-item:start key=semantic-oracle-observability-risk kind=risk_fact fact=weak_observability outcome=semantic-fact-completeness -->
Semantic completeness and implementation comparison cross a weak-observability boundary: the Harness can enforce declared census, facts, methods, identities, freshness and current results, but the soundness of authoring judgment, custom inspectors and project Oracles remains named trusted computing base and needs counterexamples plus honest blocking.
<!-- ty-source-item:end -->
